# Misses ledger

Every miss from every bench table, dated: the cases a run got wrong, the defects its audit found, the predictions
that failed, and the label errors in its own test set. Generated from `docs/bench/`, never typed, by
`node packages/verify/dist/cli.js bench index packages/verify/docs/bench`. The ledger is append-only, and this is what that means:
regenerating it refuses, and writes nothing, when a row would be dropped or changed; the only change it allows to an
existing row is recording what fixed it, and a recorded fix never changes. `bench check` also refuses a table that no
longer re-emits from its folder. Both checks read files inside this tree, so someone who edits a table's folder, the
table and this ledger together can drop a row and pass both. Only a copy outside the authors' hands would catch that,
such as a public CI history or a signed release of the ledger, and neither exists yet.
A row describes a defect in data or code, never a person. A table withdrawn from the index keeps its rows here.

Some tables record misses only, with no counts. They carry the misses of studies whose numbers sit outside the bench
rule (the AP stress study's development seeds, the SEC studies), and the defects found in rounds of cold review, in
which four cold readers (AI agents that had not worked on this tree) were each given a copy of this tree in one reader's
role (a controller, a platform engineer, a legal-operations director, a methods reviewer) and asked to break it. They
are AI agents run by the maintainers, not people and not independent reviewers. Earlier scope notes and some older rows
call them "outside readers" or "an outside reviewer"; those notes and rows are frozen, and this is what those words mean.
Some frozen scope notes also say a row is fixed when its defect is gone from "a released tree": that means the copy of
this tree handed to the next round's readers. No tagged, signed release of this tree exists yet.
A review row's description begins with the reader who logged it. Each such table's folder in `docs/bench/` holds a
scope note saying how its rows were made.
